  • Oct 10, 2026CorrectionMission Bay

    Block 18C is now named 'Mission Bay South Block 18C (UCSF Child Development Center)'. Its identification as the UCSF at Mission Bay Child Development Center, run by Bright Horizons at 727 Nelson Rising Lane, had rested on an on-the-ground observation; it is now sourced. Bright Horizons' own page and the City's business registrations place the centre at that address, the City's address point for 727 Nelson Rising Lane falls inside this shape (about 2.7 m from its edge; the nearest other mapped block is about 20 m away), and UCSF's June 2025 presentation gives the block's near-term use as a childcare center. The address point marks the street frontage, not the building, and the address also carries another business registration, so it is not exclusive to this building. No source gives an opening date. Shape, status and use are unchanged; the old page address now redirects to the new one. View record →

    Bright Horizons, UCSF at Mission Bay Child Development Center (https://www.brighthorizons.com/daycare-centers/ca/san-francisco/727-nelson-rising-lane-94143); DataSF Registered Business Locations, ttxid 1367181-07-241 (https://data.sfgov.org/resource/g8m3-pdis.json?ttxid=1367181-07-241); DataSF Addresses, address ID 743045 (https://data.sfgov.org/resource/3mea-di5p.json?eas_address_id=743045); UCSF Mission Bay Development Overview, Mission Bay CAC presentation, 2025-06-12 (https://sfocii.org/sites/default/files/2025-06/Item%202%20-%20UCSF%20Mission%20Bay%20Development%20Overview.pdf)

The ownership line now names Fifth Space, formerly Associate Capital, as the developer, per SF YIMBY, instead of describing the site as a PG&E plant.

    SF YIMBY, 2026-02-09 (https://sfyimby.com/2026/02/excavation-work-underway-for-potrero-power-station-block-2-san-francisco.html); SF Planning, Potrero Power Station project page (https://sfplanning.org/potrero-power-station); SF YIMBY, 2025-10-24 (https://sfyimby.com/2025/10/sophie-maxwell-affordable-housing-opens-in-potrero-power-station-san-francisco.html)

  • Sep 23, 2026CoverageAll sites

    DataSF's Assessor Parcel dataset gives Chase Center (parcel 8722025) and the neighboring Uber Mission Bay 3 & 4 office towers (parcel 8722063) identical geometry: one shared master-condominium-association land parcel covering the whole Mission Bay South Blocks 29-32 superblock (Chase Center, both Uber towers, a parking structure and a small broadcast building are all separate condo units on one shared plat). That made the two records' shapes overlap completely on the map, so a click anywhere in the shared area could open either record, and each record's own location map showed the wrong footprint. Both now use non-overlapping shapes built from real data: Chase Center is the full Assessor parcel with both Uber towers' OpenStreetMap building outlines subtracted, and Uber Mission Bay 3 & 4 is the union of those same two outlines. Harmonic Brewing's standalone taproom building, at the northeast edge of the site (7 Warriors Way Ste 206 per its DataSF business-registration address; the registry's geocode puts Harmonic Brewing, Miller & Lux and Cupcakin' at the same point, so it does not place any of them in a particular building), and Miller & Lux (7 Warriors Way Ste 208 per its registration address; its position inside the arena footprint is not established by the registry) are both Chase Center/Thrive City tenants, not separate structures, so Harmonic Brewing's building is included in Chase Center's new shape rather than tracked separately. Re-matching business registrations against the corrected shape turned up three more Thrive City registrations: Cupcakin' Bake Shop (Ste 200), 'Siren SF' (1 Warriors Way Unit 24, shown as Siren Cafe & Bar) and The Sarap Shop (1 Warriors Way). These are registrations at Chase Center addresses, not proof of open storefronts, and each has its own entry.

    Blocks 18B/18C corrected. A secondhand report that UCSF's own June 2025 CAC presentation (campus map updated December 2024) renumbers this row of blocks was independently checked. It showed that the building previously tracked as '18B' (a UCSF building complex west of Sandler Neurosciences Center, corrected 2026-09-20) is UCSF's 'Block 18C', labeled 'Open Space (Near term: Childcare Center)'. On review it was identified as the existing UCSF at Mission Bay Child Development Center (727 Nelson Rising Lane, operating since 2006), so 18B was renamed 18C, with the shape unchanged and the status changed to existing/interim ahead of a long-term open-space redesignation. With 18C's identity established, the 18A parking lot was extended east (~1,197 sqm) to close the access-lane gap that used to separate it from 18C, then re-split into new 18A and 18B of equal area, so the row now runs 18A/18B (parking) back-to-back with 18C (the existing childcare building). The childcare-center identification is an on-the-ground read, not independently confirmed by any source checked at the time, and is flagged as such in the record. A September 2026 accuracy check found that the UCSF deck cited (Dec 2024 map) lists Block 18C among 'undeveloped parcels remaining' with 'Open Space (Near term: Childcare Center)', which does not by itself show an existing building; no source read gives the 727 Nelson Rising Lane address or a 2006 start. The existing/interim status therefore rests on that on-the-ground read alone and needs a source.

    UCSF Mission Bay Development Overview, Mission Bay CAC presentation, 2025-06-12 (https://sfocii.org/sites/default/files/2025-06/Item%202%20-%20UCSF%20Mission%20Bay%20Development%20Overview.pdf); Bright Horizons, UCSF at Mission Bay Child Development Center (https://www.brighthorizons.com/daycare-centers/ca/san-francisco/727-nelson-rising-lane-94143)

    Block 21 split into 21A and 21B. UCSF's own June 2025 CAC presentation (campus map updated December 2024) shows that the 1625 Owens St parking lot and adjoining garage structure, added to the William J. Rutter Community Center record on 2026-09-20, are UCSF's separate 'Block 21A, Community Center Garage', distinct from the Rutter Center itself (21B), even though the two are physically connected (a rooftop parking deck joined to the community center building by a walkway). They are split along a clean straight line following the Rutter Center's real west wall (straightened from a small three-point digitizing jog to a straight edge) rather than a raw shape subtraction, which left thin sliver artifacts at the seam. The Rutter Center (21B) goes back to its original OpenStreetMap building outline; the garage (21A, a new record) is approximate, because DataSF's Building Footprints dataset does not separate the two structures, so there is no official boundary between them.

    An assumption made earlier on 2026-09-22, that blocks 33/34 were unrelated to 490/499 Illinois St, was corrected: UCSF built three structures here where the OCII plan showed two. OCII plan block 33 is UCSF's Valley Center for Vision, a second, physically distinct building from the already-tracked 490 Illinois St tower of the same name, about 60-100m east across Illinois St; how the two relate is left as an open question, not asserted as either the same facility or unrelated. OCII plan block 34 was itself two buildings, not one: it is split along a visible roofline seam in satellite imagery into the UCSF Bayfront Medical Building (north, 520 Illinois St) and the UCSF Illinois Street Garage (south, 590 Illinois St, 9 stories/500 spaces). Both were already documented in earlier research, which had guessed the split the other way round (garage as 33, clinic as 34) before an on-the-ground correction. The 33/34 boundary and the new Bayfront/Garage boundary were both redrawn from block 34's own straight east and west edges (the Illinois St and 3rd St sides, bearing about -85.6 degrees) by line intersection, rather than the original flat-latitude cut. The Mariposa St edge was left as originally traced (already within about 1 degree of the same angle).

    A second correction to the Mission Bay coverage claims, using a more rigorous method: city blocks were built from DataSF street geometry and, for every block touching the Mission Bay boundary, the share of its building-footprint area covered by a tracked record was computed. Of 58 blocks with at least 30% of their area inside the boundary, 33 have meaningful building coverage (more than 3% of block area built), and 9 of those 33 were under 50% tracked. All 9 were investigated. 500 Terry A. Francois Boulevard's map shape had captured only 1 of the 3 DataSF footprint pieces for its building (the smallest, ~1172 sq m, missing ~7460 sq m of the ~8636 sq m building) and now uses all 3. 1401 Third Street (an office building, built 2009, about 222,000 sq ft, both per the Assessor roll; tenant not identified) and the Chase Center Parking Garage (150 Warriors Way, built 2009; 469,726 sq ft per a Redfin listing, not a primary source) were added. This method is more rigorous than the satellite spot-checks and footprint-distance scans used in the two earlier checks.

  • Sep 18, 2026CorrectionAll sites

    Mission Rock labeled map: the thin street-centerline highlight was replaced with the full real street corridor after review found Blocks D1, D2 and H appeared to cover the Mission Rock Street sidewalk. Measured against DataSF's curb and centerline data, the blocks' south edge (the recorded Assessor lot line) sits about 9.5-10.3 m from the street centerline and the curb about 7.2-7.8 m, a sidewalk gap of about 2.7-3.5 m that the blocks already stop short of. No block shapes changed; only the map's drawing of the street, which had implied a much narrower street than the real right-of-way. A September 2026 accuracy check found that a quick re-measure against DataSF's curb data did not reproduce the 7.2-7.8 m curb and 2.7-3.5 m gap figures (nearest-curb distances from the centerline ran 4.6-7.4 m opposite D1/D2 and 7 m or more opposite H, mixed with crossing curbs), and the cited source has no URL; treat those figures as approximate and unconfirmed.

  • Sep 18, 2026CorrectionAll sites

    Mission Rock labeled map, second fix: the earlier street-corridor change had folded in a DataSF curb line that covers only part of Block H's Mission Rock Street frontage, pulling the drawn street edge in tight under part of H (about 1.2 m gap) while the rest of the D1/D2/H frontage kept a gap of about 3.9-4.3 m: a false pinch where the edge should be a straight line. The campus's parcel structure was checked rather than assumed: it is several large Assessor land parcels each spanning multiple buildings (the opposite of Mission Bay's condo-mapped residential towers), so building footprints are the correct, non-overlapping geometry, as with Pier 70's Building 14. One gap between Genentech Hall and Byers Hall is explained by DataSF curb data.

    OpenStreetMap building footprints; DataSF Parcels - Active and Retired (https://data.sf.gov/d/acdm-wktn); DataSF Curbs and Islands (https://data.sfgov.org/dataset/Curbs-and-Islands/emxt-b6yg)
  • Sep 18, 2026CoverageAll sites

    Record pages written for the 14 UCSF Mission Bay campus buildings added earlier (the hospital complex, 6 research and academic buildings, the community center, the utilities plant, and the 4-building student/faculty housing complex), sourced from UCSF's own press releases, architect, contractor and engineer project pages, and UCSF's historical archive. Two earlier assumptions were corrected: Sandler Neurosciences Center opened in 2012, not 2011, and the hospital's architect is Anshen+Allen/Stantec, not Perkins&Will. Unresolved discrepancies (Rock Hall's opening year, Mission Hall's street address, the Utilities Plant's construction date and address) are recorded as open questions on each record rather than picked arbitrarily.

    Skidmore, Owings & Merrill, UCSF Sandler Neurosciences Center (https://www.som.com/projects/ucsf-sandler-neurosciences-center/); Clark Construction, UCSF Sandler Neurosciences Center (https://www.clarkconstruction.com/our-work/projects/ucsf-sandler-neurosciences-center); William McDonough + Partners, UCSF Medical Center at Mission Bay (https://mcdonoughpartners.com/projects/ucsf-medical-center-at-mission-bay/); DPR Construction, UCSF Medical Center at Mission Bay (https://www.dpr.com/projects/ucsf-medical-center-at-mission-bay); A History of UCSF, 1990-2009 (https://history.library.ucsf.edu/1990.html)

  • Sep 18, 2026CoverageAll sites

    OpenStreetMap building footprints added for two of UCSF's Mission Bay parking structures (Third Street Garage, 1670 Owens Garage). A third named garage, Owens Street Garage, was found to overlap the already-mapped UCSF hospital shape by ~2,156 sq m and was left out rather than added with an unresolved conflict; it needs a satellite or site-plan check before it can be added. A fourth candidate, 450 South St, was confirmed to be a private Alexandria Real Estate garage serving its own office campus, not a UCSF building, and was not added as part of the UCSF campus.

  • Sep 18, 2026CoverageAll sites

    Five OpenStreetMap building footprints added for condo-mapped residential buildings along Berry St in Mission Bay North: Arterra (300 Berry St, ~275 units, 2009), Park Terrace (325 Berry St, 110 units, 2007), Edgewater (355 Berry St; developer, year and unit count not confirmed by any source found, left as an open question), 235 Berry (99 units, 2007) and 255 Berry (2004). The last two are Signature Properties' two Mission Bay buildings, informally 'Signature I/II' in some secondary sources but named here by address since neither carries that name in its own marketing. Mission Bay Branch Library was also added (see above). Each was checked against the official Mission Bay North boundary; several other candidates (500/550 Terry Francois Blvd, China Basin Landing, 185 Channel St) were found to be in Mission Bay South, contested or unconfirmed, and were not added. A September 2026 accuracy check found that the unit counts and years given here (Arterra ~275 units 2009, Park Terrace 110 units 2007, 235 Berry 99 units 2007, 255 Berry 2004) were taken from the records' own notes and were not independently sourced for this entry.

    OpenStreetMap way 48569995, Arterra (https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/48569995); OpenStreetMap way 46264112, Park Terrace (https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/46264112); OpenStreetMap way 46264111, Edgewater (https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/46264111); OpenStreetMap way 46264105, 235 Berry St (https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/46264105); OpenStreetMap relation 3831604, 255 Berry St (https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/3831604)
  • Sep 18, 2026CoverageAll sites

    Each of Mission Bay South's five park records now carries its OCII open-space number (P-number), marked as confirmed or inferred, tying it to OCII's own D4D/Infrastructure Plan numbering: Bayfront Park = P22 (confirmed); Mission Bay Commons = the OCII-named P11/P12/P13/P15/P16/P17 assemblage (exact sub-parcel unconfirmed); Mission Creek Park likely P1/P3; Mission Bay Commons interim west likely P12/P13/P15; Kids' Park likely P6. No community-surveyed OpenStreetMap park boundary was split or redrawn to match individual P-parcels: no source found gives P-parcel-level geometry, and OpenStreetMap's mapped boundaries reflect what is actually built. Left out as documented gaps rather than guesses: P5 (Dog Park), P7/P9 (unbuilt future sports fields), P26 (Mariposa Park) and several Port-managed waterfront parcels (P18/P19/P21/P23/P24) near Mission Rock; none had a confirmed, digitizable footprint at the time.

    OCII, Mission Bay South open space (https://sfocii.org/projects/mission-bay-south/open-space); OCII, Mission Bay Land Use Map, 2019-12 (https://sfocii.org/sites/default/files/inline-files/Att%201%20Mission%20Bay%20Land%20Use%20Map.pdf)
  • Sep 17, 2026CorrectionAll sites

    Mission Rock's eight unbuilt blocks (C, D1, D2, E, H, I, J, K) and Mission Rock Square re-traced from the adopted Design Controls' Figure 6.2. The earlier trace was cited to the Design Controls but linked to the 2012 proposal, and its transform (3-13 m residuals) had put the Square 26% over Verde and Block D1 on the 3rd Street crosswalk. The new transform is fitted to the four real Phase 1 lots (0.7 m RMS) and every block edge that meets a recorded lot line now sits on it.

    Mission Rock Design Controls (April 2018), Figure 6.2; DataSF Parcels
